# Visual audit — icml-2026-c61e72161d50

## Scope and source of truth

- **Paper:** *FlashSketch: Sketch-Kernel Co-Design for Fast Sparse Sketching on GPUs* (Rajat Vadiraj Dwaraknath, Sungyoon Kim, Mert Pilanci).
- **PDF:** `corpus/preprints/icml-2026-c61e72161d50.pdf`; verified local PDF, 51 pages, Letter 612×792 pt, PDF 1.7, 11,489,600 bytes.
- **Rendering:** all 51 pages, including references (pp. 9–10) and appendix (pp. 11–51), were rendered at 200 dpi (1700×2200 px) and inspected. Object pages were also checked in page contacts and high-resolution page renders.
- **PDF inventory:** 134 Figures + 1 Table. Algorithm 1 (p. 5) and Algorithm 2 (p. 17) are excluded because the protocol requests Figure/Table objects only. No supplementary file is present.
- **Page map:** Figures 1–4 and Table 1 are main objects (pp. 2, 5, 7–8); Figures 5–70 are Appendix F (pp. 19–35); Figures 71–134 are Appendix G (pp. 36–51).
- **Inventory reconciliation:** reading inventory and PDF captions agree on labels/pages/counts; PDF captions are the final wording authority. `spal 004` retains the PDF-visible caption spelling; source identifiers use `spal_004`.

## Source inventory, local source and GitHub

- `reports/tables/visual_source_inventory.csv` currently has an automatic `no_public_source_found` row and no selected repository; `corpus/visual_sources/icml-2026-c61e72161d50/` is absent.
- The PDF p. 2 explicitly links `https://github.com/rajatvd/flash-sketch-arxiv`; `gh repo view` confirms the exact title/arXiv paper and the `main` branch. The repository tree contains the plot generators, style modules, table writer and sketch-matrix source listed below, so source status is **exact_visual_source** after independent verification.
- No model weights, checkpoints, datasets or full repository clone were acquired. Source-exact fields below are limited to the public generator/config/style code; rendered observations are marked as mixed/rendered estimates.

## Paper-level visual style

- **Counts:** 4 main figures, 1 main table, 130 appendix figures, 0 appendix tables.
- **Typography:** figure families DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; rendered/source-mixed size range {'minimum': 5.5, 'median': 8.0, 'maximum': 11.0}; table family Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math, body/header ≈ 8.0/8.5 pt.
- **Palette:** A source-exact categorical method palette is reused across all plots: FlashSketch green #2CA02C, FlashBlockRow orange #FF7F0E, GraSS/SJLT black #000000, cuSPARSE red #D62728, FHT purple #5254A3 and Dense Gaussian blue #1F77B4. Marker shape encodes k and line style encodes (κ,s), providing some redundancy, but hue separation is not fully grayscale-safe.
- **Vector/raster:** The PDF is predominantly vector: line/bar charts, table rules, labels and captions remain vector objects. Figure 2 is the sole embedded raster image in pdfimages output (1284×642 at roughly 401 dpi); its matrix texture is combined with vector caption/text. All 51 PDF pages, including references and appendices, were rendered at 200 dpi for visual review.
- **Consistency:** PDF text uses Nimbus Roman No9 L/Computer Modern families, while matplotlib plot text is embedded as DejaVu Sans Type 3. Camera-ready plots use source min_font_size=10 with 11-point titles/axes and 9-point legends; ablation plots use the same chart grammar, 2.0-point lines, 0.8-point dispersion outlines and shared external legends. The main/appendix systems are consistent in color, marker and log-axis semantics, with the deliberate exception of the two standalone legends and Figure 4 mixed panels.

## Object-by-object audit

### Figure 1 — p. 2

- **Module/placement/width:** `introduction` / `main` / `single_column`; **types:** line, pareto; **purpose:** headline, main_comparison, efficiency_cost.
- **Complexity:** score 3/5; 1 panel(s); series=5; legend items=11; annotations=1; estimated marks=30.
- **Plot grammar:** vector rendering; x `log`, y `log`; grid `both`; legend `True` (outside right, two stacked legends); shared legend `False`; direct labels `True`; markers=6; line styles=1; hatching=False; reference lines=0; uncertainty `ellipse`; line width=2.0; provenance `mixed`.
- **Encodings:** x=Wall-clock sketch time (ms), log scale; y=Gram Frobenius error (relative), log scale; color=method family: FlashSketch green versus four baselines (blue, black, red, purple); shape=sketch dimension k: 64, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096; line=method trajectory over k; speedup annotation is an arrow/text comparison; facet=none; text=4.43x speedup annotation and title input shape.
- **Caption (40 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=True):** Figure 1. Quality–speed Pareto frontier (preview). We compare sketching methods by runtime on an NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPU (x-axis) and quality measured using error in Gram matrix approximation (y-axis). Lower is better for both axes. FlashSketch pushes the Pareto frontier.
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 5.5, 'median': 7.5, 'maximum': 11.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** categorical, 5 colors; FlashSketch (green), Dense Gaussian/cuBLAS (blue), SJLT GraSS Kernel (black), SJLT cuSPARSE (red), Subsampled Fast Hadamard Transform (purple); marker shape independently encodes k.; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=False; provenance=source_exact.
- **Data/statistics:** Representative GPT2-medium stacked-weights primitive sketching result (d=16384, n=1024). The PDF Experimental section states CUDA-event means over 10 post-warm-up iterations in FP32; the source plotting code aggregates mean points and standard-deviation ellipses. No formal interval is shown in the caption.
- **Evidence relation:** The introduction uses this preview to connect the co-design thesis to the Section 7 quality–speed claim. Figures 7–22 expand Gram approximation across inputs and shapes, while Table 1 aggregates all RandNLA tasks.
- **Design strengths:** A single log–log Pareto view makes the lower-is-better trade-off immediately legible.；The two legends separate method identity from k and the arrow makes the headline speedup visible.；Caption states hardware, metric direction, comparison and main finding.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The plot is dense for a preview and relies on color plus tiny markers at two-column size.；The caption does not define the marker/line mapping or dispersion ellipses; source and visual cross-reference are needed for full interpretation.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Use a compact log–log error/time Pareto plot with separate method and sketch-dimension legends, one explicit speedup annotation, and a caption that states metric direction.
- **Evidence:** p. 2, `Figure 1 rendered plot/diagram and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

### Figure 2 — p. 5

- **Module/placement/width:** `method` / `main` / `single_column`; **types:** qualitative_grid, matrix, conceptual_diagram; **purpose:** method_interface, theory_mechanism, experimental_design.
- **Complexity:** score 3/5; 1 panel(s); series=None; legend items=0; annotations=0; estimated marks=64.
- **Plot grammar:** raster rendering; x `none`, y `none`; grid `none`; legend `False` (none); shared legend `False`; direct labels `False`; markers=None; line styles=None; hatching=False; reference lines=0; uncertainty `none`; line width=None; provenance `source_exact`.
- **Encodings:** x=none; y=none; color=permutation identity and signed nonzero values; shape=block rectangles encode block support; no point marker semantics; line=none; facet=none; text=caption provides M, Br, Bc, κ, s, d, k and nonzero count.
- **Caption (95 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=False):** Figure 2. Example S ∼ BLOCKPERM-SJLT. Sparsity is a union of edge-disjoint permutations (degree-κ regular at the block level). Each block is a sparse JL matrix with s nonzeros per column. Here, M = 16, with block size Br = 64, Bc = 128. The block degree is κ = 4 and the intra-block sparsity is s = 2. The resulting sketch therefore has κs = 8 nonzeros per column, input dimension d = MBc = 2048 and output dimension k = MBr = 1024. Each of the 4 permutations is colored differently for visualization.
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 5.0, 'median': 8.0, 'maximum': 11.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** mixed, 7 colors; red–white–green sign matrix with translucent tab10 overlays for four edge-disjoint block permutations; block position encodes sparsity wiring; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=False; provenance=source_exact.
- **Data/statistics:** A schematic generated from a 1024×2048 sketch matrix with M=16, Br=64, Bc=128, κ=4 and s=2. The embedded matrix image shows signed nonzeros and translucent permutation overlays; it is not a statistical estimate.
- **Evidence relation:** Section 4 introduces BLOCKPERM-SJLT and Section 5 explains the thread-block kernel. This matrix interface is the visual bridge from the block-permutation construction to the local shared-memory accumulation described by Algorithm 1.
- **Design strengths:** The block support pattern makes locality and edge-disjoint permutation wiring concrete before the kernel details.；Caption is unusually self-contained: it gives construction parameters and the resulting dimensions.；Color overlays preserve a direct mapping from the four permutations to block positions.。
- **Design weaknesses:** Rasterized sign texture and translucent colors are not grayscale-safe.；The figure has no legend for permutation colors; readers must use the caption and spatial grouping rather than a keyed color label.；The rectangular matrix is conceptual and does not show the actual tile execution order or memory transactions.。
- **Reusable pattern:** For a structured random operator, show block support as a matrix first, then state the block dimensions and degree/sparsity parameters in the caption; use color as a secondary key rather than the only structural cue.
- **Evidence:** p. 5, `Figure 2 rendered plot/diagram and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

### Figure 3 — p. 7

- **Module/placement/width:** `results` / `main` / `single_column`; **types:** line, pareto; **purpose:** headline, main_comparison, efficiency_cost.
- **Complexity:** score 3/5; 1 panel(s); series=5; legend items=11; annotations=1; estimated marks=30.
- **Plot grammar:** vector rendering; x `log`, y `log`; grid `both`; legend `True` (outside right, two stacked legends); shared legend `False`; direct labels `True`; markers=6; line styles=1; hatching=False; reference lines=0; uncertainty `ellipse`; line width=2.0; provenance `mixed`.
- **Encodings:** x=Wall-clock total time (ms), log scale; y=relative sketch-and-ridge regression residual, log scale; color=method family: FlashSketch green and four baselines; shape=sketch dimension k: 64, 256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096; line=method trajectory over k; 1.83x speedup arrow/text annotation; facet=none; text=1.83x speedup annotation and Qwen2-1.5B title.
- **Caption (33 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=False):** Figure 3. Sketch-and-ridge regression on Qwen2-1.5B stacked weights. We compare end-to-end runtime on an NVIDIA RTX 4090 GPU (x-axis) and quality measured using sketch-and-ridge regression residual (y-axis). Lower is better for both axes.
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 5.5, 'median': 7.5, 'maximum': 11.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** categorical, 5 colors; FlashSketch (green), Dense Gaussian/cuBLAS (blue), SJLT GraSS Kernel (black), SJLT cuSPARSE (red), Subsampled Fast Hadamard Transform (purple); marker shape independently encodes k.; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=False; provenance=source_exact.
- **Data/statistics:** Representative Qwen2-1.5B stacked-weights sketch-and-ridge result (d=65536, n=512). The source camera-ready plot groups means by method and k and can add standard-deviation ellipses; the PDF caption states the residual and total-time directions but no interval or seed count.
- **Evidence relation:** Section 7.3 points to this object as the representative downstream Pareto frontier; Table 1 aggregates sketch-and-ridge and sketch-and-solve speedups, and Figures 39–54/G.3 expand the residual sweep.
- **Design strengths:** The same two-legend grammar as Figure 1 makes primitive and downstream comparisons visually comparable.；The explicit total-time axis distinguishes application cost from primitive sketch time.；The caption names the input, GPU, residual metric and lower-is-better direction.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The caption omits the 1.83x annotation and does not define marker/line semantics.；Residual curves and six k markers are visually crowded at the low-time end.；Shared plotting defaults make the downstream residual look like a primitive Pareto chart even though the solve pipeline changes the cost denominator.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Keep primitive and downstream quality–time figures structurally aligned, but change the x-axis label to total wall-clock time and state the downstream residual explicitly.
- **Evidence:** p. 7, `Figure 3 rendered plot/diagram and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

### Figure 4 — p. 8

- **Module/placement/width:** `results` / `main` / `single_column`; **types:** scatter, bar, pareto; **purpose:** headline, main_comparison, efficiency_cost, qualitative_evidence.
- **Complexity:** score 4/5; 2 panel(s); series=5; legend items=8; annotations=15; estimated marks=30.
- **Plot grammar:** vector rendering; x `linear`, y `linear`; grid `both`; legend `True` (outside right of top panel); shared legend `True`; direct labels `True`; markers=3; line styles=1; hatching=False; reference lines=0; uncertainty `error_bar`; line width=2.0; provenance `mixed`.
- **Encodings:** x=top: Projection time per sample (ms), linear; bottom: categorical k; y=top: LDS (higher is better); bottom: projection time per sample (ms); color=method family: FlashSketch, cuSPARSE, FHT, Dense Gaussian and GraSS; shape=top marker shape encodes k=1024, 2048, 4096; line=top method trajectories connect k values; bottom bars compare methods at k; facet=top Pareto scatter and bottom grouped bars; text=bar labels show ratios/speedups; panel titles state GraSS on MNIST and speedup context.
- **Caption (64 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=True):** Figure 4. End-to-end GraSS evaluation on MNIST. We compare sketching methods by sketch runtime per sample on an NVIDIA RTX A6000 GPU (x-axis), and quality measured using the LDS metric (y-axis). Lower is better for time (x-axis), higher is better for LDS (y-axis). FlashSketch pushes the Pareto frontier and achieves up to ∼ 3.2× speedup over the GraSS baseline while maintaining attribution quality (top).
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 5.5, 'median': 7.5, 'maximum': 11.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** categorical, 5 colors; FlashSketch (green), Dense Gaussian/cuBLAS (blue), SJLT GraSS Kernel (black), SJLT cuSPARSE (red), Subsampled Fast Hadamard Transform (purple); marker shape independently encodes k.; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=False; provenance=source_exact.
- **Data/statistics:** Two-panel GraSS-on-MNIST result (d=4k, n=512) on an NVIDIA RTX A6000. The top panel reports projection time per sample versus LDS; the bottom reports projection time bars with error bars and ratio annotations for k=1024, 2048 and 4096. The caption states the LDS direction and up-to-3.2× result.
- **Evidence relation:** Section 7.4 connects the replacement of only the GraSS projection kernel to end-to-end data attribution quality. Appendix E defines the LDS protocol; Table 1 and the RandNLA ablations provide the broader task-level cost context.
- **Design strengths:** The top Pareto panel and bottom cost bars put quality and cost in one object, matching the end-to-end claim.；Error bars and ratio text expose variability and the comparison denominator for the bar panel.；Caption states the hardware, metric directions, baseline comparison and headline speedup.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The bottom title says “Speedup relative to GraSS baseline” while its y-axis is projection time per sample; ratio labels and bar height require careful reading.；The top and bottom panels use different x semantics (continuous time versus categorical k), increasing grammar complexity.；Method color is not fully grayscale-safe even though bar position and text provide partial redundancy.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Pair a quality–cost scatter with a denominator-explicit bar panel when an end-to-end claim needs both frontier position and baseline-normalized cost; label the two x/y semantics separately.
- **Evidence:** p. 8, `Figure 4 rendered plot/diagram and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

- **Plot grammar:** vector rendering; x `none`, y `none`; grid `none`; legend `True` (center); shared legend `True`; direct labels `True`; markers=6; line styles=0; hatching=False; reference lines=0; uncertainty `none`; line width=None; provenance `source_exact`.
- **Encodings:** x=none; y=none; color=black only; shape=marker shape encodes k; line=none; facet=none; text=k values are written next to each marker.
- **Caption (19 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=False):** Figure 5. Legend for sketch dimension. Marker shapes indicate sketch dimension k for the ablation plots in this appendix.
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 7.0, 'median': 10.0, 'maximum': 12.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** grayscale, 1 colors; marker shape only: circle/square/triangle/diamond/plus/X map to k=64/256/512/1024/2048/4096; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=True; provenance=source_exact.

- **Design strengths:** Separating the six k markers from every plot materially reduces legend duplication.；Black markers and adjacent numeric labels remain readable in grayscale.；The caption explicitly scopes the key to the appendix ablations.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The legend is spatially remote from most of the 128 ablation charts.；Marker shapes such as plus and X become small after two-column scaling.；It does not show the actual method color/line-style system, so Figure 6 is required as a second lookup.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Publish one compact, standalone marker key when the same finite parameter sweep repeats across many small multiples.

- **Encodings:** x=none; y=none; color=method family; shape=none; line=κ,s parameter variants and SJLT sparsity s; facet=none; text=method and parameter labels printed beside line samples.
- **Caption (21 words; headline bold=True; self-contained=True; main finding=False):** Figure 6. Legend for methods and parameters. Line colors/styles indicate method families and (κ, s) settings used across the appendix ablations.
- **Typography:** DejaVu Sans, Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; {'minimum': 7.0, 'median': 10.0, 'maximum': 12.0}; weight=regular, bold; style=roman, italic; provenance=mixed (high).
- **Color:** categorical, 6 colors; method family color plus line style for κ,s: green FlashSketch, orange FlashBlockRow, black SJLT GraSS Kernel, red SJLT cuSPARSE, purple FHT, blue Dense Gaussian; redundant=True; grayscale-safe=False; provenance=source_exact.

- **Evidence relation:** Figure 6 completes the shared legend system with Figure 5 and decodes Figures 7–134; the main plots intentionally suppress local legends to preserve chart area.
- **Design strengths:** Color and line-style redundancy allows variant families to be compared across many plots.；The two-column key groups method family and parameter variants instead of repeating them in every panel.；Source code makes the method colors and line-style lists exact and reproducible.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The many variants create a dense key with small labels.；Hue mapping is not grayscale-safe; dashed variants are the only secondary cue.；The caption says colors/styles indicate settings but does not enumerate the exact mapping itself.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Move a stable, high-cardinality parameter legend into a dedicated appendix object, then export no-legend small multiples that share it.
- **Evidence:** p. 19, `Figure 6 legend and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

- **Design strengths:** The title and caption bind the task, input (SuiteSparse - spal 004, d=262144, n=512), hardware (NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090), and metric to the plotted object.；Log scales expose the multiplicative time/error trade-off across orders of magnitude, and marker shape plus line style separate k and (κ,s).；Mean points, connecting trajectories, and translucent dispersion ellipses preserve both Pareto direction and run variability without adding panels.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The chart has no local legend: interpreting color, line style, and marker requires Figures 5–6, which increases cross-page lookup cost.；The caption does not define the translucent standard-deviation ellipses or report the run/seed denominator; the visual evidence is therefore less self-contained than the main figures.；Two-column scaling leaves dense variants and small tick labels competing for attention, especially for the high-dimensional or SuiteSparse cases.。

- **Design weaknesses:** The chart has no local legend: interpreting color, line style, and marker requires Figures 5–6, which increases cross-page lookup cost.；The caption does not define the translucent standard-deviation ellipses or report the run/seed denominator; the visual evidence is therefore less self-contained than the main figures.；Two-column scaling leaves dense variants and small tick labels competing for attention, especially for the high-dimensional or SuiteSparse cases.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Reuse the downstream residual/time grammar from ridge regression while keeping metric labels and external parameter legends consistent across shapes and hardware.

The public plotting source groups available rows by method/configuration and k, draws mean points and connects k values; translucent ellipses encode one standard deviation in the plotted coordinates, not a confidence interval. The caption gives no seed count or failure-value convention.
- **Evidence relation:** Appendix G.4 carries the sketch-and-solve residual comparison to Synthetic Low-Rank + noise at this shape on NVIDIA RTX A6000. Figures 5–6 decode the parameter sweep and Table 1 gives the aggregate task-level speedups.
- **Design strengths:** The title and caption bind the task, input (Synthetic Low-Rank + noise, d=262144, n=512), hardware (NVIDIA RTX A6000), and metric to the plotted object.；Log scales expose the multiplicative time/error trade-off across orders of magnitude, and marker shape plus line style separate k and (κ,s).；Mean points, connecting trajectories, and translucent dispersion ellipses preserve both Pareto direction and run variability without adding panels.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The chart has no local legend: interpreting color, line style, and marker requires Figures 5–6, which increases cross-page lookup cost.；The caption does not define the translucent standard-deviation ellipses or report the run/seed denominator; the visual evidence is therefore less self-contained than the main figures.；Two-column scaling leaves dense variants and small tick labels competing for attention, especially for the high-dimensional or SuiteSparse cases.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Reuse the downstream residual/time grammar from ridge regression while keeping metric labels and external parameter legends consistent across shapes and hardware.
- **Evidence:** p. 50, `Figure 130 rendered plot and caption`, basis `rendered_observation`.

### Table 1 — p. 8

- **Module/placement/width:** `results` / `main` / `single_column`; **purpose:** headline, main_comparison, efficiency_cost.
- **Structure:** rows=4; columns=5; header levels=1; row groups=4; decimal precision=2; rules=`booktabs`; highlighting=none.
- **Caption (21 words; headline bold=False; self-contained=True; main finding=True):** Table 1. Geomean speedups of FLASHSKETCH vs baselines aggregated over shapes, datasets and configs. Global geomean vs next best baseline: 1.73x.
- **Typography:** Nimbus Roman No9 L, Computer Modern math; body/header ≈ 8.0/8.5 pt; header weight=bold; provenance=pdf_object (high).
- **Uncertainty:** No uncertainty intervals, standard deviations, ranks or failure cells are shown; entries are geometric-mean speedups.
- **Data/statistics:** Four task rows (Gram matrix approximation, OSE, sketch-and-ridge regression, Sketch+Solve) compare FlashSketch against four baseline columns: SJLT (cuSPARSE), SJLT (GraSS Kernel), Subsampled FHT and Dense Gaussian (cuBLAS). Values use two decimal places and aggregate over shapes, datasets and configurations; the caption reports the global geomean versus the next-best baseline as 1.73x.
- **Evidence relation:** Table 1 is the aggregate decision surface for the Section 7 RandNLA comparison. Figures 1 and 3 show representative quality–time frontiers, Figures 7–70/G.1–G.4 unpack task/shape variation, and Figure 4 supplies the separate GraSS end-to-end result.
- **Design strengths:** A compact five-column table puts task-level cost comparison and the global headline in one view.；Two-decimal precision is consistent across all 16 baseline cells.；Booktabs rules and cmidrules separate four task rows without a heavy grid.。
- **Design weaknesses:** The table contains speedup aggregates but no denominator counts, variability or failure indicators.；The header is single-level and long method names wrap, so baseline implementation details compete with task labels.；The global 1.73x caption claim is not reproduced in a highlighted cell, requiring a caption read.。
- **Reusable pattern:** Use a minimal booktabs aggregate table for a headline geomean, while leaving shape-level quality/time evidence to shared-grammar plots.
- **Evidence:** p. 8, `Table 1 body, header, caption and rules`, basis `rendered_observation`.

## Cross-object system

- **visual_narrative:** The visual narrative moves from the Figure 1 quality–speed claim, through the Figure 2 block-permutation interface, to the Figure 3 downstream residual frontier, Table 1 aggregate speedups and Figure 4 GraSS end-to-end quality/cost. Appendix F/G then expand each RandNLA task over 16 input shapes on RTX 4090/A6000, with Figures 5–6 acting as shared decoding keys.
- **caption_system:** Captions use a stable “Figure N. bold task/title. dataset (d,n). GPU” construction for the ablation grid and longer setup/comparison/finding prose for main objects. Main captions state metric directions and headline findings where applicable; ablation captions omit the shared legend and dispersion definitions, so they are visually complete only with Figures 5–6.
- **table_header_system:** Table 1 uses one header row: Task, SJLT (cuSPARSE), SJLT (GraSS Kernel), Subsampled FHT and Dense Gaussian (cuBLAS). Four task rows are separated by cmidrules; all cells use two decimals and no uncertainty/failure column.
- **method_result_ablation_link:** Figure 2 defines the structured sketch/kernel interface; Figure 1 and Figure 3 show representative quality–time outcomes; Table 1 and Figure 4 connect those outcomes to aggregate RandNLA and GraSS costs; F/G ablations vary task, data shape, k and (κ,s) while preserving the same visual encoding.
- **main_appendix_link:** Main objects are explicit entry points into Appendix F/G: Figure 1 points to Gram ablations, Figure 3 points to downstream residual ablations, Table 1 aggregates all four RandNLA tasks, and Figure 4 is protocol-expanded by Appendix E. Figures 5–6 provide the cross-page legend needed to read F/G.
- **typography_consistency:** Nimbus Roman/Computer Modern in paper text and DejaVu Sans in matplotlib objects are stable across main and appendix; font size compresses in the two-column PDF but source generators keep common 10–11 point chart labels and 8–9 point table/legend text.
- **color_consistency:** Method-family colors and k marker order are reused across Figures 1, 3, 4 and Figures 6–134. Line style is a second encoding for κ,s in ablations. The palette is semantically stable but not completely grayscale-safe, with Figure 2 using a separate red-white-green sign/overlay scheme.

## Final judgment

### most_reusable_patterns

- Use a two-axis log Pareto chart for multiplicative quality/time comparisons, with method color, k marker shape and one explicit speedup annotation.
- Export stable high-cardinality legends as standalone appendix figures, then use no-legend small multiples for shape/task/hardware sweeps.
- Bind every ablation caption to task, dataset, d, n and GPU while holding chart grammar constant across metrics.
- Pair an end-to-end frontier with a baseline-normalized cost bar panel when quality and operational cost are both part of the claim.

### highest_value_objects

- Figure 1: headline primitive Pareto preview.
- Figure 2: block-permutation/kernel interface schematic.
- Figure 3: downstream sketch-and-ridge Pareto preview.
- Table 1: four-task aggregate speedup surface.
- Figure 4: GraSS end-to-end LDS/cost composite.
- Figures 5–6: shared appendix decoding keys.

### failure_patterns

- The 128 ablation charts depend on remote Figures 5–6 for legend semantics; captions do not define the ellipses or variant mapping.
- A high-cardinality color/line-style system is not fully grayscale-safe and becomes small after two-column scaling.
- Table 1 omits variability, denominator counts and failure indicators, while Figure 4 mixes projection-time bars with speedup wording.
- The long appendix repeats nearly identical single-panel objects, increasing navigation cost and making cross-panel synthesis dependent on the reader.

- **one_sentence_visual_strategy:** FlashSketch uses a stable, source-reproducible method/color/marker grammar to make quality–time Pareto claims concrete in the main paper, then expands the same grammar across task, input-shape and GPU ablations with shared legends and a compact aggregate table.
